# Playlist API

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana 12.4 and later.
>
> This API complies with the new Grafana API structure. To learn more refer to documentation about the [API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
>
> **This document may not contain the latest version of the API. For the most up-to-date list of available endpoints, refer to [playlist.grafana.app/v1](https://play.grafana.org/swagger?api=playlist.grafana.app-v1) in Swagger.**

## Requirements

By default, users with the Viewer role can read playlists, and users with the Editor or Admin role can also create, update, and delete them. Users with the None role can also read playlists. If the `playlistsRBAC` feature toggle is turned on, access uses the `playlists:read` and `playlists:write` actions instead. For the related fixed roles, refer to [RBAC role definitions](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/access-control/rbac-fixed-basic-role-definitions/). For more information about the default access, refer to [Organization roles](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/#organization-roles).

## Endpoints

These are the available endpoints:

**Base path:** `/apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/`

Replace `{namespace}` with your namespace. To learn which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

| Method | Summary                                 | Path               |
| ------ | --------------------------------------- | ------------------ |
| GET    | [List Playlists](#list-playlists)       | `playlists`        |
| GET    | [Get a Playlist](#get-a-playlist)       | `playlists/{name}` |
| POST   | [Create a Playlist](#create-a-playlist) | `playlists`        |
| PUT    | [Update a Playlist](#update-a-playlist) | `playlists/{name}` |
| DELETE | [Delete a Playlist](#delete-a-playlist) | `playlists/{name}` |

## List Playlists

`GET /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/playlists`

Lists all playlists in the specified namespace.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

### Example request

The following example shows a request to list playlists:

```http
GET /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/playlists HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "PlaylistList",
  "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {},
  "items": [
    {
      "kind": "Playlist",
      "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "my-playlist-uid",
        "namespace": "default",
        "resourceVersion": "1234567890",
        "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:30:00Z"
      },
      "spec": {
        "title": "My Playlist",
        "interval": "5m",
        "items": [
          {
            "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
            "value": "dashboard-uid-1"
          },
          {
            "type": "dashboard_by_tag",
            "value": "important"
          }
        ]
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

## Get a Playlist

`GET /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/playlists/{name}`

Retrieves a specific playlist by name.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The UID of the playlist.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to get a playlist:

```http
GET /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/playlists/my-playlist-uid HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Playlist",
  "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "my-playlist-uid",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1234567890",
    "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:30:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "My Playlist",
    "interval": "5m",
    "items": [
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-1"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_tag",
        "value": "important"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Create a Playlist

`POST /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/playlists`

Creates a new playlist.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

### Example request

The following example shows a request to create a playlist:

```http
POST /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/playlists HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "kind": "Playlist",
  "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "my-new-playlist-uid"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "My New Playlist",
    "interval": "5m",
    "items": [
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-1"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_tag",
        "value": "monitoring"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 201 Created
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Playlist",
  "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "my-new-playlist-uid",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1234567891",
    "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:35:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "My New Playlist",
    "interval": "5m",
    "items": [
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-1"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_tag",
        "value": "monitoring"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Update a Playlist

`PUT /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/playlists/{name}`

Updates an existing playlist. Provide the entire playlist, including `metadata.resourceVersion` from the latest version you read.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The UID of the playlist.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to update a playlist:

```http
PUT /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/playlists/my-playlist-uid HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "kind": "Playlist",
  "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "my-playlist-uid",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1234567890"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "My Updated Playlist",
    "interval": "10m",
    "items": [
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-1"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-2"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_tag",
        "value": "updated-tag"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Playlist",
  "apiVersion": "playlist.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "my-playlist-uid",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1234567892",
    "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:30:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "My Updated Playlist",
    "interval": "10m",
    "items": [
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-1"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_uid",
        "value": "dashboard-uid-2"
      },
      {
        "type": "dashboard_by_tag",
        "value": "updated-tag"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Delete a Playlist

`DELETE /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/playlists/{name}`

Deletes a playlist.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The UID of the playlist.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to delete a playlist:

```http
DELETE /apis/playlist.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/playlists/my-playlist-uid HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Status",
  "apiVersion": "v1",
  "metadata": {},
  "status": "Success",
  "code": 200
}
```

## Playlist Item Types

Playlist items support three types:

- `dashboard_by_uid`: Include a specific dashboard by its UID
- `dashboard_by_tag`: Include all dashboards with a specific tag
- `dashboard_by_id`: (Deprecated) Include a dashboard by internal ID

## Status codes

The endpoints on this page return the following status codes:

| Code  | Meaning                                                                      | Returned by                                                                                                 |
| ----- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `200` | OK.                                                                          | [List](#list-playlists), [Get](#get-a-playlist), [Update](#update-a-playlist), [Delete](#delete-a-playlist) |
| `201` | Created.                                                                     | [Create](#create-a-playlist)                                                                                |
| `400` | Bad request (invalid JSON).                                                  | [Create](#create-a-playlist), [Update](#update-a-playlist)                                                  |
| `401` | Unauthorized.                                                                | All endpoints                                                                                               |
| `403` | Access denied.                                                               | All endpoints                                                                                               |
| `404` | Playlist not found.                                                          | [Get](#get-a-playlist), [Update](#update-a-playlist), [Delete](#delete-a-playlist)                          |
| `409` | Conflict (a playlist with the same `metadata.name` already exists).          | [Create](#create-a-playlist)                                                                                |
| `409` | Conflict (the playlist changed since you read it).                           | [Update](#update-a-playlist)                                                                                |
| `422` | Invalid playlist, for example because `metadata.resourceVersion` is missing. | [Update](#update-a-playlist)                                                                                |


# Document: Preferences HTTP API

Source path: docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/preferences.md
Pinned source: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/preferences.md
Canonical documentation: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/preferences.md
Description: Use this API to read and change user, team, and organization preferences, such as the theme, timezone, home dashboard, and language.

# Preferences HTTP API

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana 13.2 and later.
>
> This API complies with the new Grafana API structure. To learn more refer to documentation about the [API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
>
> The legacy [Preferences API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/preferences/) is still available, and removal will be announced well in advance to avoid disruptions. Refer to [Migrate from the legacy Preferences API](#migrate-from-the-legacy-preferences-api) to learn more about how both APIs match, and how to migrate.
>
> **This document may not contain the latest version of the API. For the most up-to-date list of available endpoints, refer to [preferences.grafana.app/v1](https://play.grafana.org/swagger?api=preferences.grafana.app-v1) in Swagger.**

Use this API to read and change user, team, and organization preferences, such as the theme, timezone, home dashboard, and language.

## Requirements

Access depends on who owns the preferences, not on role-based access control actions. You can read and change your own preferences. Team members can read team preferences, and users who can edit the team can change them. Every user in the organization can read organization preferences, and users with the Admin role can change them. Organization administrators can't read or change the preferences of other users. For more information, refer to [Organization preferences](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/organization-preferences/).

## Endpoints

The following table lists the main endpoints. The Swagger documentation also includes the parameters for watching changes.

**Base path:** `/apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/`

Replace `{namespace}` with your namespace. To learn which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

| Method | Summary                                           | Path                 |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- |
| GET    | [Get merged preferences](#get-merged-preferences) | `preferences/merged` |
| GET    | [List preferences](#list-preferences)             | `preferences`        |
| GET    | [Get preferences](#get-preferences)               | `preferences/{name}` |
| POST   | [Create preferences](#create-preferences)         | `preferences`        |
| PUT    | [Update preferences](#update-preferences)         | `preferences/{name}` |
| PATCH  | [Patch preferences](#patch-preferences)           | `preferences/{name}` |
| DELETE | [Delete preferences](#delete-preferences)         | `preferences/{name}` |

## Preference names and precedence

Each preferences object belongs to a user, a team, or the whole organization. Its name, `metadata.name`, identifies the owner:

| Owner        | Name              | Example         |
| ------------ | ----------------- | --------------- |
| User         | `user-{user_uid}` | `user-a1b2c3d4` |
| Team         | `team-{team_uid}` | `team-ef5gh6ij` |
| Organization | `namespace`       | `namespace`     |

`{user_uid}` and `{team_uid}` are UIDs, not numeric IDs or logins. To get your user UID, use the `uid` field returned by `GET /api/user`.

When Grafana applies preferences, it uses the first value set for each field, in this order:

1. The user preferences.
2. The preferences of the user's teams, in team UID order. Grafana only considers the first 25 teams.
3. The organization preferences.
4. The defaults in the Grafana configuration.

To get the result, use [Get merged preferences](#get-merged-preferences).

## Preferences fields

The `spec` of a preferences object has the following fields. All fields are optional. A field that isn't set falls back to the next level, as described in [Preference names and precedence](#preference-names-and-precedence).

- **theme** – The interface theme. For example, `light`, `dark`, or `system`. An empty string uses the default.
- **timezone** – A valid IANA timezone, such as `America/New_York` or `Europe/London`, `utc`, `browser`, or an empty string.
- **weekStart** – The first day of the week, in lowercase. For example, `monday`.
- **language** – The interface language. For example, `en-US`.
- **homeDashboardUID** – The UID of the home dashboard. The reserved value `global-home` uses the instance default home page instead of the home dashboard set at a lower level. An empty string clears the preference.
- **navbar.bookmarkUrls** – The URLs bookmarked in the navigation menu.
- **queryHistory.homeTab** – The default tab of the Explore query history: `query`, `starred`, or an empty string.
- **homeURL** – Read-only. The home URL from the `home_page` setting in the Grafana configuration. You can't set it through the API.

## Get merged preferences

`GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/merged`

Returns the preferences that apply to you, after combining your user, team, and organization preferences with the configured defaults.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Required permissions**: None. The response only includes the preferences that apply to you.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to get merged preferences:

```http
GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences/merged HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Preferences",
  "apiVersion": "preferences.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "resourceVersion": "1762327800000",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T07:30:00Z",
    "annotations": {
      "preferences.grafana.app/source": "user-a1b2c3d4,namespace"
    }
  },
  "spec": {
    "theme": "dark",
    "timezone": "utc",
    "weekStart": "monday",
    "language": "en-US",
    "homeDashboardUID": "jcIIG-07z"
  }
}
```

The `preferences.grafana.app/source` annotation lists the preferences objects that were combined, from highest to lowest precedence. The `resourceVersion` changes when any of them changes.

## List preferences

`GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences`

Returns the preferences objects that apply to you: your user preferences, the preferences of your first 25 teams, and the organization preferences. Objects that don't exist aren't included. Service accounts only get the organization preferences.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Query parameters**:

- **`fieldSelector`** (optional): Use `metadata.name=<NAME>` to get a single preferences object. No other field selector is supported.

The API doesn't support pagination with `continue`, or filtering with `labelSelector`.

**Required permissions**: None. The response only includes the preferences you can read.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to list preferences:

```http
GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "PreferencesList",
  "apiVersion": "preferences.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {},
  "items": [
    {
      "kind": "Preferences",
      "apiVersion": "preferences.grafana.app/v1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "user-a1b2c3d4",
        "namespace": "default",
        "resourceVersion": "1762327800000",
        "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T07:30:00Z"
      },
      "spec": {
        "theme": "dark",
        "homeDashboardUID": "jcIIG-07z"
      }
    },
    {
      "kind": "Preferences",
      "apiVersion": "preferences.grafana.app/v1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "namespace",
        "namespace": "default",
        "resourceVersion": "1762320000000",
        "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T05:20:00Z"
      },
      "spec": {
        "timezone": "utc",
        "weekStart": "monday"
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

## Get preferences

`GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/{name}`

Returns the preferences object with the given name.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: `user-{user_uid}`, `team-{team_uid}`, or `namespace`.

**Required permissions**: Read access for the owner. Refer to [Requirements](#requirements).

### Example request

The following example shows a request to get preferences:

```http
GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences/user-a1b2c3d4 HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Preferences",
  "apiVersion": "preferences.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "user-a1b2c3d4",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1762327800000",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T07:30:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "theme": "dark",
    "homeDashboardUID": "jcIIG-07z"
  }
}
```

## Create preferences

`POST /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences`

Creates the preferences for a user, a team, or the organization. You can also use [Update preferences](#update-preferences), which creates the object if it doesn't exist yet.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Required permissions**: Only access-policy tokens with explicit permissions can create preferences with `POST`. User and service account requests return `403`. Use [Update preferences](#update-preferences) or [Patch preferences](#patch-preferences) instead.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to create preferences:

```http
POST /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "metadata": {
    "name": "team-ef5gh6ij"
  },
  "spec": {
    "theme": "light",
    "homeDashboardUID": "jcIIG-07z"
  }
}
```

JSON body schema:

- **metadata.name** – Required. `user-{user_uid}`, `team-{team_uid}`, or `namespace`.
- **spec** – The preferences. For details, refer to [Preferences fields](#preferences-fields).

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 201
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Preferences",
  "apiVersion": "preferences.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "team-ef5gh6ij",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1762328000000",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T07:33:20Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "theme": "light",
    "homeDashboardUID": "jcIIG-07z"
  }
}
```

## Update preferences

`PUT /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/{name}`

Replaces the preferences with the given name. If no preferences exist for the owner yet, Grafana creates them. Fields you omit are cleared, so they fall back to the next level.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: `user-{user_uid}`, `team-{team_uid}`, or `namespace`.

**Required permissions**: Write access for the owner. Refer to [Requirements](#requirements).

### Example request

The following example shows a request to update preferences:

```http
PUT /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences/namespace HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "metadata": {
    "name": "namespace"
  },
  "spec": {
    "theme": "",
    "timezone": "utc",
    "weekStart": "monday",
    "homeDashboardUID": "jcIIG-07z"
  }
}
```

JSON body schema:

- **metadata.name** – Required. It must match `name` in the path.
- **metadata.resourceVersion** – Optional. The resource version you're updating. If the preferences changed since you read them, the request fails with `409`.
- **spec** – The complete preferences. For details, refer to [Preferences fields](#preferences-fields).

The response contains the updated preferences.

### Example response

The response contains the updated preferences. For an example, refer to the response in [Get preferences](#get-preferences).

## Patch preferences

`PATCH /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/{name}`

Updates one or more preferences without changing the others. If no preferences exist for the owner yet, Grafana creates them.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: `user-{user_uid}`, `team-{team_uid}`, or `namespace`.

**Required permissions**: Write access for the owner. Refer to [Requirements](#requirements).

### Example request

The following example shows a request to patch preferences:

```http
PATCH /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences/user-a1b2c3d4 HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/merge-patch+json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "spec": {
    "theme": "dark"
  }
}
```

### Example response

The response contains the updated preferences. For an example, refer to the response in [Get preferences](#get-preferences).

## Delete preferences

`DELETE /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/{name}`

Deletes the preferences with the given name. After that, every field falls back to the next level.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: `user-{user_uid}`, `team-{team_uid}`, or `namespace`.

**Required permissions**: Write access for the owner. Refer to [Requirements](#requirements).

### Example request

The following example shows a request to delete preferences:

```http
DELETE /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/preferences/user-a1b2c3d4 HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The response contains a `Status` object that confirms the deletion.

## Status codes

The endpoints on this page return the following status codes:

| Code  | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Returned by                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `200` | OK.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | [Get merged preferences](#get-merged-preferences), [List](#list-preferences), [Get preferences](#get-preferences), [Update](#update-preferences), [Patch](#patch-preferences), [Delete](#delete-preferences) |
| `201` | Created.                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | [Create](#create-preferences), [Update](#update-preferences), [Patch](#patch-preferences)                                                                                                                    |
| `400` | Bad request (invalid JSON).                                                                                                                                                                                                        | [Create](#create-preferences), [Update](#update-preferences), [Patch](#patch-preferences)                                                                                                                    |
| `401` | Unauthorized.                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | All endpoints                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `403` | Access denied.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | [Get merged preferences](#get-merged-preferences), [List](#list-preferences), [Get preferences](#get-preferences), [Update](#update-preferences), [Patch](#patch-preferences), [Delete](#delete-preferences) |
| `403` | Access denied. User and service account requests can't create preferences with `POST` because the path doesn't include `{name}`; use [Update preferences](#update-preferences) or [Patch preferences](#patch-preferences) instead. | [Create](#create-preferences)                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `404` | No preferences are saved for this owner.                                                                                                                                                                                           | [Get preferences](#get-preferences), [Delete](#delete-preferences)                                                                                                                                           |
| `409` | Conflict (preferences already exist for this owner).                                                                                                                                                                               | [Create](#create-preferences)                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `409` | Conflict (the preferences changed since you read them).                                                                                                                                                                            | [Update](#update-preferences), [Patch](#patch-preferences)                                                                                                                                                   |
| `422` | Invalid preferences, for example an unknown theme or timezone, or a `homeURL` value.                                                                                                                                               | [Create](#create-preferences), [Update](#update-preferences), [Patch](#patch-preferences)                                                                                                                    |

## Migrate from the legacy Preferences API

The legacy API has separate endpoints for the current user, the current organization, and teams. The new API uses one resource, and the name selects the owner:

| Legacy endpoint                      | New endpoint                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /api/user/preferences`          | `GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/user-{user_uid}`   |
| `PUT /api/user/preferences`          | `PUT /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/user-{user_uid}`   |
| `PATCH /api/user/preferences`        | `PATCH /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/user-{user_uid}` |
| `GET /api/org/preferences`           | `GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/namespace`         |
| `PUT /api/org/preferences`           | `PUT /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/namespace`         |
| `PATCH /api/org/preferences`         | `PATCH /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/namespace`       |
| `GET /api/teams/:teamId/preferences` | `GET /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/team-{team_uid}`   |
| `PUT /api/teams/:teamId/preferences` | `PUT /apis/preferences.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/preferences/team-{team_uid}`   |

When you migrate, consider the following differences:

- **Team preferences:** The legacy [team preferences endpoints](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/team/) use the numeric team ID. The new API uses the team UID.
- **Home dashboard:** `homeDashboardId` isn't available. Use `homeDashboardUID`.
- **Effective preferences:** The legacy API has no equivalent of [Get merged preferences](#get-merged-preferences).
- **Responses:** Updates return the saved preferences object instead of a `{"message":"Preferences updated"}` message.

Canonical documentation: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/queries.md
Description: Use this API to manage saved queries, also known as the query library. A saved query stores one or more data source queries with a title, description, and tags, so you and your team can reuse them in Explore and dashboa…

# Queries HTTP API

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana 12.2 and later.
>
> This API complies with the new Grafana API structure. To learn more refer to documentation about the [API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
>
> The legacy [Query history API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/query_history/) is still available. This API is related to it, but it isn't a full replacement. Refer to [Differences from the legacy Query history API](#differences-from-the-legacy-query-history-api) to learn more about how both APIs match.
>
> **This document may not contain the latest version of the API. For the most up-to-date list of available endpoints, refer to [queries.grafana.app/v1](https://play.grafana.org/swagger?api=queries.grafana.app-v1) in Swagger.**

Use this API to manage [saved queries](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/visualizations/panels-visualizations/query-transform-data/#saved-queries), also known as the query library. A saved query stores one or more data source queries with a title, description, and tags, so you and your team can reuse them in Explore and dashboards.

## Before you begin

Saved queries are only available in Grafana Enterprise and Grafana Cloud.

## Requirements

By default, users with the Viewer role can read saved queries, and users with the Editor role can also create, update, and delete them. For the related actions and fixed roles, refer to [RBAC role definitions](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/access-control/rbac-fixed-basic-role-definitions/).

## Endpoints

The following table lists the main endpoints. The Swagger documentation also includes endpoints to search saved queries, delete a collection of saved queries, apply partial updates with `PATCH`, and render a saved query with its template variables replaced.

**Base path:** `/apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/`

Replace `{namespace}` with your namespace. To learn which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

| Method | Summary                                       | Path             |
| ------ | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------- |
| GET    | [List saved queries](#list-saved-queries)     | `queries`        |
| GET    | [Get a saved query](#get-a-saved-query)       | `queries/{name}` |
| POST   | [Create a saved query](#create-a-saved-query) | `queries`        |
| PUT    | [Update a saved query](#update-a-saved-query) | `queries/{name}` |
| DELETE | [Delete a saved query](#delete-a-saved-query) | `queries/{name}` |

## List saved queries

`GET /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries`

Returns the saved queries in the organization. Use the `limit` query parameter to control the maximum number of saved queries returned. To retrieve more saved queries, use the `continue` token from the response to fetch the next page.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Query parameters**:

- **`limit`** (optional): Maximum number of saved queries to return.
- **`continue`** (optional): Continue token from a previous response to fetch the next page.
- **`labelSelector`** (optional): Return only saved queries whose labels match the selector.

**Required permissions**: `queries:read`

### Example request

The following example shows a request to list saved queries:

```http
GET /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/queries?limit=1 HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "QueryList",
  "apiVersion": "queries.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "continue": "eyJvIjoxNTIsInYiOjE3NjE3MDQyMjQyMDcxODksInMiOmZhbHNlfQ=="
  },
  "items": [
    {
      "kind": "Query",
      "apiVersion": "queries.grafana.app/v1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "cpu-usage-by-instance",
        "namespace": "default",
        "uid": "65327fce-c545-489d-ada5-16f909453d12",
        "resourceVersion": "1762325460000",
        "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z",
        "annotations": {
          "grafana.app/createdBy": "user:u000000001"
        }
      },
      "spec": {
        "title": "CPU usage by instance",
        "description": "Non-idle CPU usage, grouped by instance",
        "tags": ["cpu", "node"],
        "isVisible": true,
        "isLocked": false,
        "targets": [
          {
            "variables": {},
            "properties": {
              "refId": "A",
              "datasource": {
                "type": "prometheus",
                "uid": "prometheus-uid"
              },
              "expr": "sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!=\"idle\"}[5m]))"
            }
          }
        ]
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

Continue making requests with the updated `continue` token until you receive a response without a `continue` field in the metadata, which means you've reached the last page.

## Get a saved query

`GET /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries/{name}`

Returns the saved query with the given name.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the saved query. This is the `metadata.name` field in the saved query response.

**Required permissions**: `queries:read`

### Example request

The following example shows a request to get a saved query:

```http
GET /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/queries/cpu-usage-by-instance HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Query",
  "apiVersion": "queries.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "cpu-usage-by-instance",
    "namespace": "default",
    "uid": "65327fce-c545-489d-ada5-16f909453d12",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325460000",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z",
    "annotations": {
      "grafana.app/createdBy": "user:u000000001"
    }
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "CPU usage by instance",
    "description": "Non-idle CPU usage, grouped by instance",
    "tags": ["cpu", "node"],
    "isVisible": true,
    "isLocked": false,
    "targets": [
      {
        "variables": {},
        "properties": {
          "refId": "A",
          "datasource": {
            "type": "prometheus",
            "uid": "prometheus-uid"
          },
          "expr": "sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!=\"idle\"}[5m]))"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Create a saved query

`POST /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries`

Creates a saved query.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Required permissions**: `queries:write`

### Example request

The following example shows a request to create a saved query:

```http
POST /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/queries HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "metadata": {
    "generateName": "cpu-"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "CPU usage by instance",
    "description": "Non-idle CPU usage, grouped by instance",
    "tags": ["cpu", "node"],
    "isVisible": true,
    "isLocked": false,
    "targets": [
      {
        "variables": {},
        "properties": {
          "refId": "A",
          "datasource": {
            "type": "prometheus",
            "uid": "prometheus-uid"
          },
          "expr": "sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!=\"idle\"}[5m]))"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

JSON body schema:

- **metadata.name** – The unique identifier of the saved query. If you don't want to provide one, set `metadata.generateName` to the prefix you want, and Grafana generates the rest of the name.
- **spec.title** – The title of the saved query.
- **spec.description** – A description of the saved query. Use an empty string if you don't want a description.
- **spec.tags** – A list of tags to organize and find the saved query. Use an empty list if you don't want tags.
- **spec.isVisible** – Whether the saved query is visible in the query library.
- **spec.isLocked** – Whether the saved query is shown as locked in the UI. This flag controls UI display only. Permissions are enforced separately.
- **spec.targets** – The data source queries to save.
  - **properties** – The query, in the format the data source plugin uses. Include `refId` and `datasource`.
  - **variables** – Where to replace template variables in the query. Use an empty object if the query doesn't use template variables.
- **spec.vars** – Optional. The template variables the saved query uses, each with a `key` and optional `defaultValues`.

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 201
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Query",
  "apiVersion": "queries.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "cpu-7xkq2",
    "namespace": "default",
    "uid": "a1f3c2e4-9b7d-4c1e-8f2a-3d5b6c7e8f90",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325460000",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z",
    "annotations": {
      "grafana.app/createdBy": "service-account:cef2t2rfm73lsb"
    }
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "CPU usage by instance",
    "description": "Non-idle CPU usage, grouped by instance",
    "tags": ["cpu", "node"],
    "isVisible": true,
    "isLocked": false,
    "targets": [
      {
        "variables": {},
        "properties": {
          "refId": "A",
          "datasource": {
            "type": "prometheus",
            "uid": "prometheus-uid"
          },
          "expr": "sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!=\"idle\"}[5m]))"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Update a saved query

`PUT /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries/{name}`

Replaces an existing saved query identified by name. Provide the full object, including `metadata.resourceVersion` from the latest version of the saved query.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the saved query.

**Required permissions**: `queries:write`

### Example request

The following example shows a request to update a saved query:

```http
PUT /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/queries/cpu-usage-by-instance HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "kind": "Query",
  "apiVersion": "queries.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "cpu-usage-by-instance",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325460000"
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "CPU usage by instance",
    "description": "Non-idle CPU usage over 1 minute, grouped by instance",
    "tags": ["cpu", "node"],
    "isVisible": true,
    "isLocked": true,
    "targets": [
      {
        "variables": {},
        "properties": {
          "refId": "A",
          "datasource": {
            "type": "prometheus",
            "uid": "prometheus-uid"
          },
          "expr": "sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!=\"idle\"}[1m]))"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Query",
  "apiVersion": "queries.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "cpu-usage-by-instance",
    "namespace": "default",
    "uid": "65327fce-c545-489d-ada5-16f909453d12",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325999000",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z",
    "annotations": {
      "grafana.app/createdBy": "user:u000000001",
      "grafana.app/updatedBy": "service-account:cef2t2rfm73lsb",
      "grafana.app/updatedTimestamp": "2026-11-05T07:00:00Z"
    }
  },
  "spec": {
    "title": "CPU usage by instance",
    "description": "Non-idle CPU usage over 1 minute, grouped by instance",
    "tags": ["cpu", "node"],
    "isVisible": true,
    "isLocked": true,
    "targets": [
      {
        "variables": {},
        "properties": {
          "refId": "A",
          "datasource": {
            "type": "prometheus",
            "uid": "prometheus-uid"
          },
          "expr": "sum by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!=\"idle\"}[1m]))"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Delete a saved query

`DELETE /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries/{name}`

Deletes an existing saved query identified by name. This operation can't be reverted.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the saved query.

**Required permissions**: `queries:write`

### Example request

The following example shows a request to delete a saved query:

```http
DELETE /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/queries/cpu-usage-by-instance HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Status",
  "apiVersion": "v1",
  "metadata": {},
  "status": "Success",
  "code": 200
}
```

## Status codes

The endpoints on this page return the following status codes:

| Code  | Meaning                                                                                                        | Returned by                                                                                                              |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `200` | OK.                                                                                                            | [List](#list-saved-queries), [Get](#get-a-saved-query), [Update](#update-a-saved-query), [Delete](#delete-a-saved-query) |
| `201` | Created.                                                                                                       | [Create](#create-a-saved-query)                                                                                          |
| `400` | Bad request (invalid JSON).                                                                                    | [Create](#create-a-saved-query), [Update](#update-a-saved-query)                                                         |
| `401` | Unauthorized.                                                                                                  | All endpoints                                                                                                            |
| `403` | Access denied.                                                                                                 | [List](#list-saved-queries), [Get](#get-a-saved-query), [Delete](#delete-a-saved-query)                                  |
| `403` | Access denied, for example if you don't have query access to all referenced data sources.                      | [Create](#create-a-saved-query)                                                                                          |
| `403` | Access denied, for example if you don't have query access to all referenced data sources in the updated query. | [Update](#update-a-saved-query)                                                                                          |
| `404` | Saved query not found, or you don't have query access to all referenced data sources.                          | [Get](#get-a-saved-query), [Delete](#delete-a-saved-query)                                                               |
| `404` | Saved query not found, or you don't have query access to all referenced data sources in the existing query.    | [Update](#update-a-saved-query)                                                                                          |
| `409` | Conflict (a saved query with the same `metadata.name` already exists).                                         | [Create](#create-a-saved-query)                                                                                          |
| `409` | Conflict (the saved query changed since you read it).                                                          | [Update](#update-a-saved-query)                                                                                          |

## Differences from the legacy Query history API

The Saved queries API and the legacy [Query history API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/query_history/) both store data source queries on the server, but they serve different purposes:

- **Query history** records the queries each user runs in Explore, so they can find them again later.
- **Saved queries** are queries you choose to save, with a title, description, and tags, and share with your organization.

If you use the Query history API to keep queries on the server, you can use the Saved queries API instead. The following table shows how the endpoints match:

| Legacy endpoint                                  | Saved queries endpoint                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `POST /api/query-history`                        | `POST /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries`          |
| `GET /api/query-history`                         | `GET /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries`           |
| `PATCH /api/query-history/:uid`                  | `PATCH /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries/{name}`  |
| `DELETE /api/query-history/:uid`                 | `DELETE /apis/queries.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/queries/{name}` |
| `POST` and `DELETE /api/query-history/star/:uid` | No equivalent                                                               |

Consider the following differences:

- **Availability:** Saved queries are only available in Grafana Enterprise and Grafana Cloud.
- **Request body:** The legacy `datasourceUid` and `queries` fields map to `spec.targets`, where each target's `properties` contains one query, including its `datasource`. The legacy `comment` field maps to `spec.description`.
- **Search:** The legacy search parameters, such as `searchString`, `sort`, `onlyStarred`, and `from`/`to`, have no direct equivalent in the list endpoint. Use the search endpoint documented in Swagger to filter saved queries.
- **Stars:** Saved queries don't have a starred state.

# Resource History HTTP API

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana 12.0 and later.
>
> This API complies with the new Grafana API structure. To learn more refer to documentation about the [API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
>
> The legacy [Dashboard Versions API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/dashboard_versions/) is still available, and removal will be announced well in advance to avoid disruptions. Refer to [Migrate from the legacy Dashboard Versions API](#migrate-from-the-legacy-dashboard-versions-api) to learn more about how both APIs match, and how to migrate.
>
> **This document may not contain the latest version of the API. For the most up-to-date list of available endpoints, refer to the specification of each API in [Swagger](https://play.grafana.org/swagger).**

The new Grafana APIs track version history for resources. You can retrieve the history of any resource by using the standard List endpoint with specific query parameters. This page documents how to list resource history, using dashboards as an example. The same pattern applies to any resource.

**Resource history is available on all stable (GA) API versions (for example, `v1`, `v2`)**. Alpha or beta versions may not support it.

## Requirements

To read the history of a resource, you need permission to read that resource. For the permissions each resource requires, refer to the requirements on its API page, for example the [Dashboard API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/dashboard/#requirements).

## Endpoints

These are the available endpoints:

**Base path:** `/apis/{group}/{version}/namespaces/{namespace}/`

Replace `{group}` with the API group of the resource, for example `dashboard.grafana.app`, and `{version}` with the API version of the resource, for example `v1`. Replace `{namespace}` with your namespace. To learn which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

| Method | Summary                                                     | Path                                                                                       |
| ------ | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| GET    | [List resource history](#list-resource-history)             | `{resource}?labelSelector=grafana.app/get-history=true&fieldSelector=metadata.name=<NAME>` |
| GET    | [Retrieve a specific version](#retrieve-a-specific-version) | `{resource}/{name}?resourceVersion=latest`                                                 |

## List resource history

`GET /apis/{group}/{version}/namespaces/{namespace}/{resource}?labelSelector=grafana.app/get-history=true&fieldSelector=metadata.name=<NAME>`

Lists the version history of a specific resource. This uses the standard List endpoint with two required query parameters.

**Path parameters**:

- `{group}`: The API group of the resource. For example, `dashboard.grafana.app`.
- `{version}`: The API version of the resource. For example, `v1`.
- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{resource}`: The resource type, in plural. For example, `dashboards`.

**Query parameters**:

- **`labelSelector`** (required): Must be set to `grafana.app/get-history=true` to request history instead of a normal list.
- **`fieldSelector`** (required): Must be set to `metadata.name=<NAME>` to identify the specific resource. `<NAME>` is the `metadata.name` field of the resource (the Grafana UID).
- **`limit`** (optional): Maximum number of history entries to return per page.
- **`continue`** (optional): Token from a previous response to fetch the next page.

History entries are returned in reverse chronological order (newest first).

### Example request

The following request retrieves the version history for a dashboard with `metadata.name` of `production-overview` in the `default` namespace:

```http
GET /apis/dashboard.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/dashboards?labelSelector=grafana.app/get-history=true&fieldSelector=metadata.name=production-overview HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "DashboardList",
  "apiVersion": "dashboard.grafana.app/v1",
  "metadata": {
    "resourceVersion": "1758777451428472",
    "continue": "eyJvIjoxNTIsInYiOjE3NjE3MDQyMjQyMDcxODksInMiOmZhbHNlfQ=="
  },
  "items": [
    {
      "kind": "Dashboard",
      "apiVersion": "dashboard.grafana.app/v1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "production-overview",
        "namespace": "default",
        "uid": "a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-g1h2-i3j4k5l6m7n8",
        "resourceVersion": "1758777451428472",
        "generation": 3,
        "creationTimestamp": "2026-03-01T08:00:00Z",
        "annotations": {
          "grafana.app/updatedBy": "user:u000000001",
          "grafana.app/updatedTimestamp": "2026-03-15T14:22:10Z",
          "grafana.app/message": "Added latency panel"
        }
      },
      "spec": {
        "title": "Production Overview",
        "schemaVersion": 41,
        ...
      }
    },
    {
      "kind": "Dashboard",
      "apiVersion": "dashboard.grafana.app/v1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "production-overview",
        "namespace": "default",
        "uid": "a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-g1h2-i3j4k5l6m7n8",
        "resourceVersion": "1758777451428100",
        "generation": 2,
        "creationTimestamp": "2026-03-01T08:00:00Z",
        "annotations": {
          "grafana.app/updatedBy": "user:u000000001",
          "grafana.app/updatedTimestamp": "2026-03-10T09:15:30Z",
          "grafana.app/message": "Updated thresholds"
        }
      },
      "spec": {
        "title": "Production Overview",
        "schemaVersion": 41,
        ...
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

Each item in the `items` array represents a historical version of the resource. The following metadata fields are especially relevant for history:

- **`metadata.generation`**: The version number. This increments each time the resource changes, for example when its spec changes or it moves to another folder.
- **`metadata.resourceVersion`**: A unique identifier for this specific version. Use this for pagination and change detection.
- **`metadata.creationTimestamp`**: When the resource was first created. This value is the same in every version.
- **`metadata.annotations.grafana.app/updatedTimestamp`**: When this specific version was saved. The first version doesn't have this annotation, so use `metadata.creationTimestamp` instead.
- **`metadata.annotations.grafana.app/updatedBy`**: The user who saved this version, in the format `<user-type>:<uid>`.
- **`metadata.annotations.grafana.app/message`**: An optional commit message set during the update. You can set this annotation when [updating a resource](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developers/http_api/dashboard/#update-dashboard).
- **`spec`**: The full resource spec as it existed at that version.

## Pagination

You can paginate through history using `limit` and `continue`, the same way you paginate a standard List request.

### Example request with pagination

The following example shows a request that returns two history entries per page:

```http
GET /apis/dashboard.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/dashboards?labelSelector=grafana.app/get-history=true&fieldSelector=metadata.name=production-overview&limit=2 HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

If the response includes a `metadata.continue` field, pass it as the `continue` query parameter in the next request to fetch the next page. When the response doesn't include a `continue` field, you've reached the last page.

### Example request for the next page

The following example shows a request for the next page, using the `continue` token from the previous response:

```http
GET /apis/dashboard.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/dashboards?labelSelector=grafana.app/get-history=true&fieldSelector=metadata.name=production-overview&limit=2&continue=eyJvIjoxNTIsInYiOjE3NjE3MDQyMjQyMDcxODksInMiOmZhbHNlfQ== HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

## Retrieve a specific version

To retrieve the full resource at a specific historical version, use the standard Get endpoint with the `resourceVersion` query parameter set to the `metadata.resourceVersion` from the history list.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to retrieve a specific version of a dashboard:

```http
GET /apis/dashboard.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/default/dashboards/production-overview?resourceVersion=1758777451428100 HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

This returns the complete resource as it existed at that version. For an example of a dashboard response, refer to [Get Dashboard](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/dashboard/#get-dashboard).

## Status codes

The endpoints on this page return the following status codes:

| Code  | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                      | Returned by                              |
| ----- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| `200` | OK.                                                                                                                                                                          | All endpoints                            |
| `400` | Bad request (missing `fieldSelector`, invalid `labelSelector`).                                                                                                              | [List](#list-resource-history)           |
| `400` | The resource version is newer than the latest version, when Grafana uses the SQLKV storage backend. The default SQL backend returns the latest available version with `200`. | [Retrieve](#retrieve-a-specific-version) |
| `401` | Unauthorized.                                                                                                                                                                | All endpoints                            |
| `403` | Access denied.                                                                                                                                                               | All endpoints                            |
| `404` | Resource or version not found.                                                                                                                                               | [Retrieve](#retrieve-a-specific-version) |

## Migrate from the legacy Dashboard Versions API

This API replaces the legacy [Dashboard Versions API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/dashboard_versions/) (`/api/dashboards/uid/:uid/versions`). The legacy API only works with dashboards, while this API works with any resource on a stable API version.

The following table describes how the legacy endpoints map to this API:

| Legacy endpoint                                  | Resource history endpoint                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /api/dashboards/uid/:uid/versions`          | `GET /apis/dashboard.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/dashboards?labelSelector=grafana.app/get-history=true&fieldSelector=metadata.name=<UID>` |
| `GET /api/dashboards/uid/:uid/versions/:version` | `GET /apis/dashboard.grafana.app/v1/namespaces/{namespace}/dashboards/<UID>?resourceVersion=latest`                                                 |

Consider the following differences:

- **Version identifier:** The legacy API identifies a version by its `version` number, which is the same value as `metadata.generation`. This API uses `metadata.resourceVersion` to retrieve a specific version. To find the `resourceVersion` of a version number, list the history and find the item whose `metadata.generation` matches that number.
- **Pagination:** The legacy `start` query parameter has no equivalent. The legacy `continueToken` query parameter and response field map to the `continue` query parameter and the `metadata.continue` field. For details, refer to [Pagination](#pagination).
- **Response format:** Each history item is a full resource object. The version details are in `metadata`, and the dashboard is in `spec`.
- **Users:** The legacy `createdBy` field contains the user's login. This API returns the user in the format `<user-type>:<uid>`.
- **Messages:** The legacy list endpoint replaces the message of the first version with `Initial save`. This API returns the stored message, which can be empty.

The following table describes how the legacy response fields map to this API:

| Legacy field    | Resource history field                                                                                                    |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`            | `metadata.generation`                                                                                                     |
| `uid`           | `metadata.name`                                                                                                           |
| `dashboardId`   | `metadata.labels.grafana.app/deprecatedInternalID`                                                                        |
| `version`       | `metadata.generation`                                                                                                     |
| `parentVersion` | No equivalent. The value is `metadata.generation` minus 1.                                                                |
| `restoredFrom`  | No equivalent. If the version is a restore, `metadata.annotations.grafana.app/message` is `Restored from version latest`. |
| `created`       | `metadata.annotations.grafana.app/updatedTimestamp`, or `metadata.creationTimestamp` if the annotation isn't set          |
| `createdBy`     | `metadata.annotations.grafana.app/updatedBy`, or `metadata.annotations.grafana.app/createdBy` for the first version       |
| `message`       | `metadata.annotations.grafana.app/message`                                                                                |
| `data`          | `spec`                                                                                                                    |


# Document: Secrets Management HTTP API
