Canonical documentation: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/secrets_management.md
Description: The Grafana Secrets Management API allows you to manage secrets that are used by other services and applications within your Grafana instance.

# Secrets Management API

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana 12.2 and later.
>
> This API complies with the new Grafana API structure. To learn more refer to documentation about the [API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
>
> **This document may not contain the latest version of the API. For the most up-to-date list of available endpoints, refer to [secret.grafana.app/v1beta1](https://play.grafana.org/swagger?api=secret.grafana.app-v1beta1) in Swagger.**
>
> This API is in `beta`, so it might change in future releases.

The Grafana Secrets Management API allows you to manage secrets that are used by other services and applications within your Grafana instance.

## Requirements

If you're running Grafana Enterprise or Grafana Cloud, some endpoints require specific permissions. For more information, refer to [Role-based access control permissions](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/access-control/custom-role-actions-scopes/).

## Endpoints

These are the available endpoints:

**Base path:** `/apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/`

Replace `{namespace}` with your namespace. To learn which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

| Method | Summary                                         | Path                  |
| ------ | ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------- |
| POST   | [Create a secure value](#create-a-secure-value) | `securevalues`        |
| GET    | [List secure values](#list-secure-values)       | `securevalues`        |
| GET    | [Get a secure value](#get-a-secure-value)       | `securevalues/{name}` |
| PUT    | [Update a secure value](#update-a-secure-value) | `securevalues/{name}` |
| DELETE | [Delete a secure value](#delete-a-secure-value) | `securevalues/{name}` |

## Decrypters

The decrypters field is an allowlist that lets the secure value know which services and apps can decrypt the secret value.

Currently available decrypters:

- `k6-cloud` (for Grafana Cloud k6)
- `provisioning.grafana.app` (for GitSync/Provisioning)
- `synthetic-monitoring` (for Synthetic Monitoring checks)

## Create a secure value

`POST /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/securevalues`

Creates a new secure value.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Request body**:

- `metadata.name`: The Grafana unique identifier. If you do not want to provide this, set `metadata.generateName` instead to the prefix you would like for the randomly generated `uid` (can't be an empty string).
- `spec.description`: Short description that explains the purpose of this secure value. Required. Up to 25 characters long.
- `spec.value`: The secret value to store. Required. Up to 24576 bytes long.
- `spec.decrypters`: List of services allowed to decrypt this secure value. Up to 64 items, see note in [decrypters](#decrypters) for a list of supported values.

**Required permissions**:

See note in the [introduction](#secrets-management-api) for an explanation.

| Action                       | Scope                                     |
| ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| `secret.securevalues:create` | <ul><li>`secret.securevalues:*`</li></ul> |
| { .no-spacing-list }         |                                           |

### Example request

The following example shows a request to create a secure value:

```http
POST /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/securevalues HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "metadata": {
    "name": "api-key"
  },
  "spec": {
    "description": "External API Key",
    "value": "secret-api-key-12345",
    "decrypters": ["synthetic-monitoring"]
  }
}
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 201 Created
Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8
Content-Length: 343

{
  "apiVersion": "secret.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "kind": "SecureValue",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "api-key",
    "namespace": "default",
    "uid": "f1e2d3c4-b5a6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890",
    "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:35:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "description": "External API Key",
    "decrypters": ["synthetic-monitoring"]
  },
  "status": {}
}
```

> **Note**
>
> The `spec.value` field is never returned by API endpoints. Users can't decrypt secrets.

## List secure values

`GET /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/securevalues`

List all secure values in a namespace.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Query parameters**:

- `labelSelector`: Filter secure values by labels.

**Required permissions**:

See note in the [introduction](#secrets-management-api) for an explanation.

| Action                     | Scope                                     |
| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| `secret.securevalues:read` | <ul><li>`secret.securevalues:*`</li></ul> |
| { .no-spacing-list }       |                                           |

### Example request

The following example shows a request to list secure values:

```http
GET /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/securevalues HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8
Content-Length: 383

{
  "apiVersion": "secret.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "kind": "SecureValueList",
  "metadata": {
    "resourceVersion": "12345"
  },
  "items": [
    {
      "apiVersion": "secret.grafana.app/v1beta1",
      "kind": "SecureValue",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "database-password",
        "namespace": "default",
        "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:30:00Z"
      },
      "spec": {
        "description": "Production DB Password",
        "decrypters": ["synthetic-monitoring"]
      },
      "status": {}
    }
  ]
}
```

## Get a secure value

`GET /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/securevalues/{name}`

Get the details of a specific secure value. It will not return the secret value.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The name of the secure value.

**Required permissions**:

See note in the [introduction](#secrets-management-api) for an explanation.

| Action                     | Scope                                     |
| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| `secret.securevalues:read` | <ul><li>`secret.securevalues:*`</li></ul> |
| { .no-spacing-list }       |                                           |

### Example request

The following example shows a request to get a secure value:

```http
GET /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/securevalues/api-key HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8
Content-Length: 294

{
  "apiVersion": "secret.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "kind": "SecureValue",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "api-key",
    "namespace": "default",
    "uid": "f1e2d3c4-b5a6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890",
    "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:35:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "description": "External API Key",
    "decrypters": ["synthetic-monitoring"]
  },
  "status": {}
}
```

## Update a secure value

`PUT /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/securevalues/{name}`

Replace an existing secure value with a new specification.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The name of the secure value.

**Request body**:

- `spec.description`: Short description that explains the purpose of this secure value. Required. Up to 25 characters long.
- `spec.value`: The secret value to store. Required. Up to 24576 bytes long.
- `spec.decrypters`: List of services allowed to decrypt this secure value. Up to 64 items, see note in [decrypters](#decrypters) for a list of supported values.

**Required permissions**:

See note in the [introduction](#secrets-management-api) for an explanation.

| Action                      | Scope                                     |
| --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| `secret.securevalues:write` | <ul><li>`secret.securevalues:*`</li></ul> |
| { .no-spacing-list }        |                                           |

### Example request

The following example shows a request to update a secure value:

```http
PUT /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/securevalues/api-key HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "metadata": {
    "name": "api-key"
  },
  "spec": {
    "description": "External API Key",
    "value": "new-value-12345",
    "decrypters": ["synthetic-monitoring"]
  }
}
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8
Content-Length: 282

{
  "apiVersion": "secret.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "kind": "SecureValue",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "api-key",
    "namespace": "default",
    "uid": "f1e2d3c4-b5a6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890",
    "creationTimestamp": "2024-01-15T10:35:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "description": "External API Key",
    "decrypters": ["synthetic-monitoring"]
  }
}
```

## Delete a secure value

`DELETE /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/securevalues/{name}`

Permanently delete a secure value. This also deletes the underlying stored secret value.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The name of the secure value.

**Required permissions**:

See note in the [introduction](#secrets-management-api) for an explanation.

| Action                       | Scope                                     |
| ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| `secret.securevalues:delete` | <ul><li>`secret.securevalues:*`</li></ul> |
| { .no-spacing-list }         |                                           |

### Example request

The following example shows a request to delete a secure value:

```http
DELETE /apis/secret.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/securevalues/api-key HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8
Content-Length: 65

{
  "apiVersion": "v1",
  "kind": "Status",
  "status": "Success",
  "code": 200
}
```

## Status codes

The endpoints on this page return the following status codes:

| Code  | Meaning                                                                                                      | Returned by                                                                                                                 |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `200` | OK.                                                                                                          | [List](#list-secure-values), [Get](#get-a-secure-value), [Update](#update-a-secure-value), [Delete](#delete-a-secure-value) |
| `201` | Created.                                                                                                     | [Create](#create-a-secure-value)                                                                                            |
| `400` | Bad request (invalid JSON).                                                                                  | [Create](#create-a-secure-value), [Update](#update-a-secure-value)                                                          |
| `401` | Unauthorized.                                                                                                | All endpoints                                                                                                               |
| `403` | Access denied.                                                                                               | All endpoints                                                                                                               |
| `404` | Not found.                                                                                                   | [Get](#get-a-secure-value), [Update](#update-a-secure-value), [Delete](#delete-a-secure-value)                              |
| `409` | Conflict (a secure value with the same name already exists).                                                 | [Create](#create-a-secure-value)                                                                                            |
| `422` | Invalid secure value, for example because a required field is missing or a field value isn't valid.          | [Create](#create-a-secure-value)                                                                                            |
| `422` | Invalid secure value, for example because a field value isn't valid or you switch between `value` and `ref`. | [Update](#update-a-secure-value)                                                                                            |


# Document: Short URL HTTP API

Source path: docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/short_url.md
Pinned source: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/short_url.md
Canonical documentation: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/short_url.md
Description: Use this API to create shortened URLs. A short URL represents a longer URL containing complex query parameters in a smaller and simpler format.

# Short URL HTTP API

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana 12.4 and later.
>
> This API complies with the new Grafana API structure. To learn more refer to documentation about the [API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
>
> The legacy [Short URL API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/short_url/) is still available, and removal will be announced well in advance to avoid disruptions. Refer to [Migrate from the legacy Short URL API](#migrate-from-the-legacy-short-url-api) to learn more about how both APIs match, and how to migrate.
>
> **This document may not contain the latest version of the API. For the most up-to-date list of available endpoints, refer to [shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1](https://play.grafana.org/swagger?api=shorturl.grafana.app-v1beta1) in Swagger.**
>
> This API is in `beta`, so it might change in future releases.

Use this API to create shortened URLs. A short URL represents a longer URL containing complex query parameters in a smaller and simpler format.

## Requirements

Short URLs don't use role-based access control. Any signed-in user, including users with the None role, can create, get, and resolve short URLs. Listing, updating, and deleting short URLs requires the Admin role. Listing is restricted to admins because it exposes every short URL in the organization. Updating and deleting are restricted because they can change or remove short URLs that other users created. For more information, refer to [Organization roles](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/#organization-roles).

## Endpoints

The following table lists the main endpoints. The Swagger documentation also includes endpoints to search short URLs, delete a collection of short URLs, apply partial updates with `PATCH`, and read or update the `status` subresource.

**Base path:** `/apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/`

Replace `{namespace}` with your namespace. To learn which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

| Method | Summary                                     | Path                    |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| GET    | [List short URLs](#list-short-urls)         | `shorturls`             |
| GET    | [Get a short URL](#get-a-short-url)         | `shorturls/{name}`      |
| GET    | [Resolve a short URL](#resolve-a-short-url) | `shorturls/{name}/goto` |
| POST   | [Create a short URL](#create-a-short-url)   | `shorturls`             |
| PUT    | [Update a short URL](#update-a-short-url)   | `shorturls/{name}`      |
| DELETE | [Delete a short URL](#delete-a-short-url)   | `shorturls/{name}`      |

## List short URLs

`GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls`

Returns all short URLs in the organization. Use the `limit` query parameter to control the maximum number of short URLs returned. To retrieve more short URLs, use the `continue` token from the response to fetch the next page.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Query parameters**:

- **`limit`** (optional): Maximum number of short URLs to return.
- **`continue`** (optional): Continue token from a previous response to fetch the next page.

**Required permissions**: Admin role.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to list short URLs:

```http
GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "ShortURLList",
  "apiVersion": "shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "metadata": {},
  "items": [
    {
      "kind": "ShortURL",
      "apiVersion": "shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1",
      "metadata": {
        "name": "AT76wBvGk",
        "namespace": "default",
        "resourceVersion": "1762325460",
        "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z"
      },
      "spec": {
        "path": "d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=1599389322894&to=1599410922894"
      },
      "status": {
        "lastSeenAt": 0
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

## Get a short URL

`GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls/{name}`

Returns the short URL with the given name.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the short URL. This is the `metadata.name` field in the short URL response.

**Required permissions**: Any authenticated user.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to get a short URL:

```http
GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls/AT76wBvGk HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "ShortURL",
  "apiVersion": "shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "AT76wBvGk",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325460",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "path": "d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=1599389322894&to=1599410922894"
  },
  "status": {
    "lastSeenAt": 1762325712000
  }
}
```

The response contains the following fields:

- **metadata.name** – The unique identifier of the short URL.
- **spec.path** – The path the short URL links to, relative to the Grafana [`root_url`](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/setup-grafana/configure-grafana/#root_url).
- **status.lastSeenAt** – The last time someone used the short URL, in milliseconds since the Unix epoch. The initial value is `0`.

## Resolve a short URL

`GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls/{name}/goto`

Redirects to the full URL that the short URL links to. Grafana builds the full URL from its [`root_url`](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/setup-grafana/configure-grafana/#root_url) and the `spec.path` of the short URL, and updates `status.lastSeenAt`.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the short URL.

**Query parameters**:

- **`redirect`** (optional): Set to `false` to return the full URL in a JSON body instead of redirecting.

**Required permissions**: Any authenticated user.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to resolve a short URL:

```http
GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls/AT76wBvGk/goto HTTP/1.1
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 302 Found
Location: https://grafana.example.com/d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=1599389322894&to=1599410922894
```

### Example request with `redirect=false`

The following example shows a request with `redirect=false`, which returns the full URL in the response body instead of redirecting:

```http
GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls/AT76wBvGk/goto?redirect=false HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "url": "https://grafana.example.com/d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=1599389322894&to=1599410922894"
}
```

## Create a short URL

`POST /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls`

Creates a short URL.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

**Required permissions**: Any authenticated user.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to create a short URL:

```http
POST /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "metadata": {
    "generateName": "dash-"
  },
  "spec": {
    "path": "d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=1599389322894&to=1599410922894"
  }
}
```

JSON body schema:

- **metadata.name** – The unique identifier of the short URL. If you don't want to provide one, set `metadata.generateName` to the prefix you want, and Grafana generates the rest of the name.
- **spec.path** – The path to shorten, relative to the Grafana [`root_url`](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/setup-grafana/configure-grafana/#root_url). For example, `d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard` or `explore?from=123&to=456`.

The path must be relative. Grafana rejects the following paths:

- Absolute paths that start with `/`
- Paths that contain `../` or `..\`
- Paths that start with `//` or `\`
- Paths that contain a URL scheme, such as `https://` or `javascript:`

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 201
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "ShortURL",
  "apiVersion": "shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "dash-7xkq2",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325460",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "path": "d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=1599389322894&to=1599410922894"
  },
  "status": {
    "lastSeenAt": 0
  }
}
```

To share the short URL, use `<GRAFANA_URL>/goto/<NAME>?orgId=<ORG_ID>`, where `<NAME>` is the `metadata.name` from the response.

## Update a short URL

`PUT /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls/{name}`

Replaces an existing short URL identified by name. Provide the full object, including `metadata.resourceVersion` from the latest version of the short URL.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the short URL.

**Required permissions**: Admin role.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to update a short URL:

```http
PUT /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls/AT76wBvGk HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "kind": "ShortURL",
  "apiVersion": "shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "AT76wBvGk",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325460"
  },
  "spec": {
    "path": "d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=now-6h&to=now"
  }
}
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "ShortURL",
  "apiVersion": "shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1",
  "metadata": {
    "name": "AT76wBvGk",
    "namespace": "default",
    "resourceVersion": "1762325999",
    "creationTimestamp": "2026-11-05T06:51:00Z"
  },
  "spec": {
    "path": "d/TxKARsmGz/new-dashboard?orgId=1&from=now-6h&to=now"
  },
  "status": {
    "lastSeenAt": 1762325712000
  }
}
```

## Delete a short URL

`DELETE /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls/{name}`

Deletes an existing short URL identified by name. This operation can't be reverted.

**Path parameters**:

- `{namespace}`: To learn more about which namespace to use, refer to the [API overview](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).
- `{name}`: The unique identifier of the short URL.

**Required permissions**: Admin role.

### Example request

The following example shows a request to delete a short URL:

```http
DELETE /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/default/shorturls/AT76wBvGk HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>
```

### Example response

The following example shows the response:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "kind": "Status",
  "apiVersion": "v1",
  "metadata": {},
  "status": "Success",
  "code": 200
}
```

## Status codes

The endpoints on this page return the following status codes:

| Code  | Meaning                                                                                                  | Returned by                                                                                                     |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `200` | OK.                                                                                                      | [List](#list-short-urls), [Get](#get-a-short-url), [Update](#update-a-short-url), [Delete](#delete-a-short-url) |
| `200` | OK, when `redirect=false`.                                                                               | [Resolve](#resolve-a-short-url)                                                                                 |
| `201` | Created.                                                                                                 | [Create](#create-a-short-url)                                                                                   |
| `201` | Created, if the short URL doesn't exist and the request body doesn't include `metadata.resourceVersion`. | [Update](#update-a-short-url)                                                                                   |
| `302` | Found, redirects to the full URL.                                                                        | [Resolve](#resolve-a-short-url)                                                                                 |
| `400` | Bad request (invalid JSON or invalid `metadata.name`).                                                   | [Create](#create-a-short-url), [Update](#update-a-short-url)                                                    |
| `401` | Unauthorized.                                                                                            | All endpoints                                                                                                   |
| `403` | Access denied (only organization administrators can list short URLs).                                    | [List](#list-short-urls)                                                                                        |
| `403` | Access denied.                                                                                           | [Get](#get-a-short-url), [Resolve](#resolve-a-short-url)                                                        |
| `403` | Access denied, or the path is invalid.                                                                   | [Create](#create-a-short-url)                                                                                   |
| `403` | Access denied (only organization administrators can update short URLs), or the path is invalid.          | [Update](#update-a-short-url)                                                                                   |
| `403` | Access denied (only organization administrators can delete short URLs).                                  | [Delete](#delete-a-short-url)                                                                                   |
| `404` | Short URL not found.                                                                                     | [Get](#get-a-short-url), [Resolve](#resolve-a-short-url), [Delete](#delete-a-short-url)                         |
| `409` | Conflict (a short URL with the same name already exists).                                                | [Create](#create-a-short-url)                                                                                   |
| `409` | Conflict (the short URL changed or was deleted since you read it).                                       | [Update](#update-a-short-url)                                                                                   |

## Migrate from the legacy Short URL API

The new API uses different request and response shapes than the legacy `/api/short-urls` endpoints, so changing the URL alone isn't enough. The following table shows how the endpoints match:

| Legacy endpoint            | New endpoint                                                                          |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `POST /api/short-urls`     | `POST /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls`            |
| `GET /api/short-urls/:uid` | `GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls/{name}`      |
| `GET /goto/:uid`           | `GET /apis/shorturl.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/shorturls/{name}/goto` |

When you migrate, consider the following differences:

- **Request body:** The legacy `path` and `uid` fields move to `spec.path` and `metadata.name`.
- **Response body:** The legacy API returns `uid` and a ready-to-share `url`. The new API returns the full `ShortURL` object, so build the shareable URL from `metadata.name` as described in [Create a short URL](#create-a-short-url).
- **New operations:** The new API also supports listing, updating, and deleting short URLs, which require the Admin role.

# Legacy Grafana HTTP API

> **Note**
>
> Starting in Grafana 13, `/api` endpoints are being deprecated in favor of the `/apis` route. Note that while Grafana is working on migrating existing APIs, currently there may not be an exact match to the legacy API you're using.
>
> **This change doesn't disrupt or break your current setup**. Legacy APIs are not being disabled and remain fully accessible and operative, but `/api` routes will no longer be updated.
>
> To learn more refer to the [new API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

The following legacy APIs are available:

# Document: Admin HTTP API

Source path: docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin.md
Pinned source: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin.md

Description: To use the Admin API endpoints you have to use Basic authentication, and the Grafana user must have the Grafana server administrator permission. The admin user that Grafana is provisioned with by default has permissions…

# Admin API

> **Note**
>
> Starting in Grafana 13, `/api` endpoints are being deprecated in favor of the `/apis` route. Note that while Grafana is working on migrating existing APIs, currently there may not be an exact match to the legacy API you're using.
>
> **This change doesn't disrupt or break your current setup**. Legacy APIs are not being disabled and remain fully accessible and operative, but `/api` routes will no longer be updated.
>
> To learn more refer to the [new API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

## Requirements

**To use the Admin API endpoints you have to use Basic authentication, and the Grafana user must have the Grafana server administrator permission**. The `admin` user that Grafana is provisioned with by default has permissions to use these API endpoints.

You can't authenticate to the Admin HTTP API with service account tokens. Service accounts are limited to an organization and an organization role, and can't be granted [Grafana server administrator permissions](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/#grafana-server-administrators).

If you're running Grafana Enterprise, for some endpoints you'll need to have specific permissions. Refer to [Role-based access control permissions](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/access-control/custom-role-actions-scopes/) for more information.

## Fetch settings

`GET /api/admin/settings`

**Required permissions**

Only works with Basic Authentication (username and password). Refer to [Requirements](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin#requirements) for more information.

| Action        | Scope                                                                               |
| ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| settings:read | settings:\**<br>settings:auth.saml:*<br>settings:auth.saml:enabled (property level) |

**Example Request**:

GET /api/admin/settings
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json

## Update settings

`PUT /api/admin/settings`

> **Note**
>
> Available in Grafana Enterprise v8.0+.

Updates/removes and reloads database settings. You must provide either `updates`, `removals` or both.

This endpoint only supports changes to `auth.saml` configuration.

This endpoint writes to Grafana's settings table, where values override the configuration file, environment variables, and command line arguments until you remove them with `removals`. Grafana gives no indication that a stored value is in effect, so a credential rotation done by editing the configuration file silently keeps using the old value. The SSO Settings API, the user interfaces, and Terraform write a different table; for SAML, `DELETE /api/v1/sso-settings/saml` clears both. Refer to [Check for stored settings](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/setup-grafana/configure-grafana/settings-updates-at-runtime/#check-for-stored-settings).

**Required permissions**

Only works with Basic Authentication (username and password). Refer to [Requirements](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin#requirements) for more information.

| Action         | Scope                                                                               |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| settings:write | settings:\**<br>settings:auth.saml:*<br>settings:auth.saml:enabled (property level) |

**Example request:**

```http
PUT /api/admin/settings
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer <SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN>

{
  "updates": {
    "auth.saml": {
      "enabled": "true"
    }
  },
  "removals": {
    "auth.saml": ["single_logout"]
  },
}
```

**Example response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json
Content-Length: 32

{
  "message":"Settings updated"
}
```

Status codes:

- **200** - OK
- **400** - Bad Request
- **401** - Unauthorized
- **403** - Forbidden
- **500** - Internal Server Error

## Global Users

`POST /api/admin/users`

Creates new user.

**Required permissions**

Only works with Basic Authentication (username and password). Refer to [Requirements](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin#requirements) for more information.

| Action       | Scope |
| ------------ | ----- |
| users:create | n/a   |

**Example Request**:

```http
POST /api/admin/users HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "name":"User",
  "email":"user@graf.com",
  "login":"user",
  "password":"userpassword",
  "OrgId": 1
}
```

Note that `OrgId` is an optional parameter that can be used to assign a new user to a different organization when [auto\_assign\_org](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/setup-grafana/configure-grafana/#auto-assign-org) is set to `true`.

**Example Response**:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{"id":5,"message":"User created"}
```

## Revoke auth token for User

`POST /api/admin/users/:id/revoke-auth-token`

Revokes the given auth token (device) for the user. User of issued auth token (device) will no longer be logged in
and will be required to authenticate again upon next activity.

**Required permissions**

Only works with Basic Authentication (username and password). Refer to [Requirements](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin#requirements) for more information.

| Action                | Scope           |
| --------------------- | --------------- |
| users.authtoken:write | global.users:\* |

**Example Request**:

```http
POST /api/admin/users/1/revoke-auth-token HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "authTokenId": 364
}
```

**Example Response**:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "message": "User auth token revoked"
}
```

## Logout User

`POST /api/admin/users/:id/logout`

Logout user revokes all auth tokens (devices) for the user. User of issued auth tokens (devices) will no longer be logged in
and will be required to authenticate again upon next activity.

**Required permissions**

Only works with Basic Authentication (username and password). Refer to [Requirements](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin#requirements) for more information.

| Action       | Scope           |
| ------------ | --------------- |
| users.logout | global.users:\* |

**Example Request**:

```http
POST /api/admin/users/1/logout HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
```

**Example Response**:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "message": "User auth token revoked"
}
```

## Reload provisioning configurations

`POST /api/admin/provisioning/dashboards/reload`

`POST /api/admin/provisioning/datasources/reload`

`POST /api/admin/provisioning/plugins/reload`

`POST /api/admin/provisioning/access-control/reload`

`POST /api/admin/provisioning/alerting/reload`

Reloads the provisioning config files for specified type and provision entities again. It won't return
until the new provisioned entities are already stored in the database. In case of dashboards, it will stop
polling for changes in dashboard files and then restart it with new configurations after returning.

**Required permissions**

Only works with Basic Authentication (username and password). Refer to [Requirements](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/admin#requirements) for more information.

| Action              | Scope                      | Provision entity |
| ------------------- | -------------------------- | ---------------- |
| provisioning:reload | provisioners:accesscontrol | accesscontrol    |
| provisioning:reload | provisioners:dashboards    | dashboards       |
| provisioning:reload | provisioners:datasources   | datasources      |
| provisioning:reload | provisioners:plugins       | plugins          |
| provisioning:reload | provisioners:alerting      | alerting         |

**Example Request**:

```http
POST /api/admin/provisioning/dashboards/reload HTTP/1.1
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
```

**Example Response**:

```http
HTTP/1.1 200
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "message": "Dashboards config reloaded"
}
```

## Reload LDAP configuration

**This endpoint has been removed from the Admin API. Starting with Grafana 12.4.0, SSO settings automatically reload the LDAP settings from the database or from the .toml file.**

`POST /api/admin/ldap/reload`

**Example Response**:

```http
HTTP/1.1 410
Content-Type: application/json

{"message":"This endpoint has been removed. LDAP configuration is now loaded from SSO settings."}
```

# Document: Alerting Provisioning HTTP API

Source path: docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/alerting_provisioning.md
Pinned source: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/alerting_provisioning.md
Canonical documentation: https://github.com/grafana/grafana/blob/b8e90ef2a99c8629339e7233a7a813456d87c075/docs/sources/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/api-legacy/alerting_provisioning.md
Description: The Alerting Provisioning HTTP API can be used to create, modify, and delete resources relevant to Grafana-managed alerts. This API is the one used by our Grafana Terraform provider.

# Alerting provisioning HTTP API

> **Note**
>
> Starting in Grafana 13, `/api` endpoints are being deprecated in favor of the `/apis` route. Note that while Grafana is working on migrating existing APIs, currently there may not be an exact match to the legacy API you're using.
>
> **This change doesn't disrupt or break your current setup**. Legacy APIs are not being disabled and remain fully accessible and operative, but `/api` routes will no longer be updated.
>
> To learn more refer to the [new API structure in Grafana](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/apis/).

> **Note**
>
> This API is partially replaced by two new HTTP APIs:
>
> - **[Alert rules HTTP API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/alert-rules/):** Replaces the alert rule and rule group endpoints. Refer to [Migrate from the legacy Alerting Provisioning API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/alert-rules/#migrate-from-the-legacy-alerting-provisioning-api) to learn how both APIs match.
> - **[Alert notifications HTTP API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/alert-notifications/):** Replaces the contact points, notification policies, notification template groups, and mute timings endpoints. Refer to [Migrate from the legacy Alerting Provisioning API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/alert-notifications/#migrate-from-the-legacy-alerting-provisioning-api) to learn how both APIs match.
>
> The file-provisioning `export` endpoints and receiver permission endpoints don't have equivalents in the new APIs yet and remain available here.

The Alerting Provisioning HTTP API can be used to create, modify, and delete resources relevant to Grafana-managed alerts. This API is the one used by our [Grafana Terraform provider](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/grafana/grafana/latest/docs).

> If you are running Grafana Enterprise, you need to add specific permissions for some endpoints. For more information, refer to [Role-based access control permissions](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/administration/roles-and-permissions/access-control/custom-role-actions-scopes/).

> **Warning**
>
> This API is partially replaced by the [Alert rules HTTP API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/alert-rules/) (for alert rules, recording rules, and rule groups) and the [Alert notifications HTTP API](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/developer-resources/api-reference/http-api/alert-notifications/) (for contact points, notification policies, notification template groups, and mute timings, including staged Alertmanager configurations). Those legacy endpoints are deprecated and will be removed in a future release. The file-provisioning `export` endpoints and receiver permission endpoints remain on this API.

## Grafana-managed endpoints

> **Note**
>
> In the Alerting provisioning HTTP API, the endpoints use a JSON format that differs from the format returned by the `export` endpoints.
>
> The `export` endpoints allow you to export alerting resources in a JSON format suitable for [provisioning via files](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/v13.3/alerting/set-up/provision-alerting-resources/file-provisioning/). However, this format cannot be used to update resources via the HTTP API.

### Alert rules

The following endpoints can be used to manage both alert rules and recording rules. To create a recording rule, include a `record` block in your request instead of a `condition` field.

| Method | URI                                                              | Name                                        | Summary                                                 |
| ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| DELETE | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules/:uid                            | [route delete alert rule](#route)           | Delete a specific alert rule by UID.                    |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules/:uid                            | [route get alert rule](#route)              | Get a specific alert rule by UID.                       |
| POST   | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules                                 | [route post alert rule](#route)             | Create a new alert rule.                                |
| PUT    | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules/:uid                            | [route put alert rule](#route)              | Update an existing alert rule.                          |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules/:uid/export                     | [route get alert rule export](#route)       | Export an alert rule in provisioning file format.       |
| DELETE | /api/v1/provisioning/folder/:folderUid/rule-groups/:group        | [route delete alert rule group](#route)     | Delete a rule group.                                    |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/folder/:folderUid/rule-groups/:group        | [route get alert rule group](#route)        | Get a rule group.                                       |
| PUT    | /api/v1/provisioning/folder/:folderUid/rule-groups/:group        | [route put alert rule group](#route)        | Create or update a rule group.                          |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/folder/:folderUid/rule-groups/:group/export | [route get alert rule group export](#route) | Export an alert rule group in provisioning file format. |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules                                 | [route get alert rules](#route)             | Get all the alert rules.                                |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules/export                          | [route get alert rules export](#route)      | Export all alert rules in provisioning file format.     |

**Example request for new alert rule:**

#### Recording rules

Recording rules allow you to pre-compute frequently used or computationally expensive queries and save the results as a new time series metric. The same alert rule provisioning endpoints support creating recording rules by including the `record` field instead of alert-specific fields like `condition`, `noDataState`, or `execErrState`.

**Example request for new recording rule:**

```http
POST /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk

{
  "title": "my_recording_rule",
  "ruleGroup": "recording_rules_group",
  "folderUID": "SET_FOLDER_UID",
  "for": "0s",
  "orgId": 1,
  "uid": "",
  "labels": {
    "team": "backend"
  },
  "data": [
    {
      "refId": "A",
      "queryType": "",
      "relativeTimeRange": {
        "from": 600,
        "to": 0
      },
      "datasourceUid": "PROMETHEUS_DATASOURCE_UID",
      "model": {
        "expr": "sum(rate(http_requests_total[5m]))",
        "intervalMs": 1000,
        "maxDataPoints": 43200,
        "refId": "A"
      }
    }
  ],
  "record": {
    "metric": "http_requests:rate5m:sum",
    "from": "A",
    "target_datasource_uid": "TARGET_PROMETHEUS_UID"
  }
}
```

**Example response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 201 Created
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "id": 2,
  "uid": "YYYYYYYYY",
  "orgID": 1,
  "folderUID": "SET_FOLDER_UID",
  "ruleGroup": "recording_rules_group",
  "title": "my_recording_rule",
  "for": "0s",
  "data": [
    {
      "refId": "A",
      "queryType": "",
      "relativeTimeRange": {
        "from": 600,
        "to": 0
      },
      "datasourceUid": "PROMETHEUS_DATASOURCE_UID",
      "model": {
        "expr": "sum(rate(http_requests_total[5m]))",
        "intervalMs": 1000,
        "maxDataPoints": 43200,
        "refId": "A"
      }
    }
  ],
  "updated": "2024-08-02T14:30:15.123456789Z",
  "labels": {
    "team": "backend"
  },
  "provenance": "api",
  "isPaused": false,
  "record": {
    "metric": "http_requests:rate5m:sum",
    "from": "A",
    "target_datasource_uid": "TARGET_PROMETHEUS_UID"
  }
}
```

**Important notes for recording rules:**

- The `metric` field must be a valid Prometheus metric name and contain no whitespace.
- The `from` field specifies which query reference (refId) to use as the source for the recorded metric.
- The `target_datasource_uid` specifies which Prometheus-compatible data source to write the results to. If not specified, the default data source configured in `[recording_rules].default_datasource_uid` is used.
- Recording rules do not support `condition`, `noDataState`, `execErrState`, or `notification_settings` fields.
- Set `for` to `0s` for recording rules as they do not have a pending state.

### Contact points

The contact point endpoints support only v1 integrations. `GET` requests omit integrations that use another version. `PUT` and `DELETE` requests for those integrations return `404 Not Found`.

| Method | URI                                        | Name                                     | Summary                                                |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| DELETE | /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points/:uid   | [route delete contactpoints](#route)     | Delete a contact point.                                |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points        | [route get contactpoints](#route)        | Get all the contact points.                            |
| POST   | /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points        | [route post contactpoints](#route)       | Create a contact point.                                |
| PUT    | /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points/:uid   | [route put contactpoint](#route)         | Update an existing contact point.                      |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points/export | [route get contactpoints export](#route) | Export all contact points in provisioning file format. |

**Example Request for all the contact points:**

```http
GET /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk
```

**Example Response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

[
  {
    "uid": "",
    "name": "email receiver",
    "type": "email",
    "settings": {
      "addresses": "<example@example.com>"
    },
    "disableResolveMessage": false
  }
]
```

### Receiver permissions

The receiver permissions endpoints manage access control for contact point receivers. These endpoints allow you to assign permissions to users, teams, or built-in roles for specific receivers.

| Method | URI                                                          | Name                                            | Summary                                                     |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| POST   | /api/access-control/receivers/:uid/users/:userID             | [route set user receiver permission](#route)    | Set permissions for a user on a specific receiver.          |
| POST   | /api/access-control/receivers/:uid/teams/:teamID             | [route set team receiver permission](#route)    | Set permissions for a team on a specific receiver.          |
| POST   | /api/access-control/receivers/:uid/builtInRoles/:builtInRole | [route set builtin receiver permission](#route) | Set permissions for a built-in role on a specific receiver. |

**Example Request to assign permissions to a user:**

```http
POST /api/access-control/receivers/abc123/users/5
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk

{
  "permission": "Edit"
}
```

**Example Response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "message": "Permission updated"
}
```

**Example Request to remove permissions from a team:**

```http
POST /api/access-control/receivers/abc123/teams/3
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk

{
  "permission": ""
}
```

**Example Response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "message": "Permission removed"
}
```

**Example Request to assign permissions to a built-in role:**

```http
POST /api/access-control/receivers/abc123/builtInRoles/Viewer
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk

{
  "permission": "View"
}
```

**Example Response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "message": "Permission updated"
}
```

**Available Permissions sets:**

- `View` - Read-only access to the receiver.
  - Grants `alert.notifications.receivers:read`.
- `Edit` - Ability to update and test the receiver.
  - Grants `View` actions plus:
    - `alert.notifications.receivers:write`
    - `alert.notifications.receivers:delete`
    - `alert.notifications.receivers.test:create`
- `Admin` - Full access including managing permissions and reading secrets.
  - Grants `Edit` actions plus:
    - `alert.notifications.receivers.secrets:read`
    - `receivers.permissions:read`
    - `receivers.permissions:write`
    - `alert.notifications.receivers.protected:write`
- Empty string (`""`) - Removes the permission.

### Notification policies

| Method | URI                                  | Name                                   | Summary                                                          |
| ------ | ------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| DELETE | /api/v1/provisioning/policies        | [route reset policy tree](#route)      | Clears the notification policy tree.                             |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/policies        | [route get policy tree](#route)        | Get the notification policy tree.                                |
| PUT    | /api/v1/provisioning/policies        | [route put policy tree](#route)        | Sets the notification policy tree.                               |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/policies/export | [route get policy tree export](#route) | Export the notification policy tree in provisioning file format. |

**Example Request for exporting the notification policy tree in YAML format:**

```http
GET /api/v1/provisioning/policies/export?format=yaml
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk
```

**Example Response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: text/yaml

apiVersion: 1
policies:
    - orgId: 1
      receiver: My Contact Email Point
      group_by:
        - grafana_folder
        - alertname
      routes:
        - receiver: My Contact Email Point
          object_matchers:
            - - monitor
              - =
              - testdata
          mute_time_intervals:
            - weekends
```

### Notification template groups

Template groups enable you to define multiple notification templates (`{{ define "" }}`) within a single group. They can be managed from the Grafana Alerting UI.

| Method | URI                                  | Name                            | Summary                                         |
| ------ | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| DELETE | /api/v1/provisioning/templates/:name | [route delete template](#route) | Delete a notification template group.           |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/templates/:name | [route get template](#route)    | Get a notification template group.              |
| GET    | /api/v1/provisioning/templates       | [route get template](#route)    | Get all notification template groups.           |
| PUT    | /api/v1/provisioning/templates/:name | [route put template](#route)    | Create or update a notification template group. |

**Example Request for all notification template groups:**

```http
GET /api/v1/provisioning/templates
Accept: application/json
Content-Type: application/json
Authorization: Bearer eyJrIjoiT0tTcG1pUlY2RnVKZTFVaDFsNFZXdE9ZWmNrMkZYbk
```

**Example Response:**

```http
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Content-Type: application/json

[
  {
    "name": "custom_email.message",
    "template": "{{ define \"custom_email.message\" }}\n  Custom alert!\n{{ end }}",
    "provenance": "file"
  },
  {
    "name": "custom_email.subject",
    "template": "{{ define \"custom_email.subject\" }}\n{{ len .Alerts.Firing }} firing alert(s), {{ len .Alerts.Resolved }} resolved alert(s)\n{{ end }}",
    "provenance": "file"
  }
]
```

### Edit resources in the Grafana UI

By default, you cannot edit API-provisioned alerting resources in Grafana.

To enable editing these resources in the Grafana UI, add the **`X-Disable-Provenance: true`** header to the following API requests:

- [`PUT /api/v1/provisioning/folder/{FolderUID}/rule-groups/{Group}`](#route): This action also sets the provenance for the rule group and all its alert rules.
- [`POST /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules`](#route): The provenance of the new alert rule must match the provenance value configured for its rule group.
- [`POST /api/v1/provisioning/contact-points`](#route)
- [`POST /api/v1/provisioning/mute-timings`](#route)
- [`PUT /api/v1/provisioning/templates/{name}`](#route)
- [`PUT /api/v1/provisioning/policies`](#route)

To reset the notification policy tree to the default and unlock it for editing in the Grafana UI, use:

- [`DELETE /api/v1/provisioning/policies`](#route)

## Data source-managed resources

> **Caution**
>
> Pre-provisioned Loki and Prometheus data source-managed alerts have been deprecated in Grafana Cloud and can not be created in new stacks.
> New Grafana Cloud stacks will use Grafana-managed alerting (GMA) by default. Datasource-managed alerting (DMA) is not provisioned in new stacks. Existing stacks are not affected.
>
> This applies to the default Loki and Prometheus Grafana Cloud data sources managed by Grafana Labs and Cloud Alertmanager, which will not be available nor will Grafana provision the data source for it. If you add your own Mimir, Loki, or Alertmanager data sources, you can continue to use data source-managed alerts.
>
> Cloud users can import DMA rules into GMA rules with the import tool. See the [import data source-managed alerts to Grafana-managed alerts documentation](https://grafana.com/docs/grafana/latest/alerting/alerting-rules/alerting-migration/) for information on how to do this.

## Paths

### <span id="route-delete-alert-rule"></span> Delete a specific alert rule by UID. (*RouteDeleteAlertRule*)

```
DELETE /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules/:uid
```

> **Warning**
>
> This API is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Use the [Grafana App Platform alerting APIs](https://editor.swagger.io/?url=https://raw.githubusercontent.com/grafana/grafana/main/packages/grafana-openapi/src/apis/rules.alerting.grafana.app-v1beta1.json) instead: `/apis/rules.alerting.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/alertrules/{name}` for alert rules and `/apis/rules.alerting.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/recordingrules/{name}` for recording rules.

#### Parameters

| Name                         | Source | Type   | Go type | Required | Default | Description                                               |
| ---------------------------- | ------ | ------ | ------- | :------: | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| `UID`                        | path   | string | string  |     ✓    |         | Alert rule UID                                            |
| `X-Disable-Provenance: true` | header | string | string  |          |         | Allows editing of provisioned resources in the Grafana UI |

#### All responses

| Code          | Status     | Description                              | Has headers | Schema           |
| ------------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------- | :---------: | ---------------- |
| [204](#route) | No Content | The alert rule was deleted successfully. |             | [schema](#route) |

#### Responses

##### <span id="route-delete-alert-rule-204"></span> 204 - The alert rule was deleted successfully.

Status: No Content

###### <span id="route-delete-alert-rule-204-schema"></span> Schema

#### Parameters

#### All responses

### <span id="route-delete-mute-timing"></span> Delete a mute timing. (*RouteDeleteMuteTiming*)

```
DELETE /api/v1/provisioning/mute-timings/:name
```

> **Warning**
>
> This API is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Use the [Grafana App Platform alerting APIs](https://editor.swagger.io/?url=https://raw.githubusercontent.com/grafana/grafana/main/packages/grafana-openapi/src/apis/notifications.alerting.grafana.app-v1beta1.json) instead: `/apis/notifications.alerting.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/timeintervals/{name}` for mute timings.

#### Parameters

| Name      | Source | Type   | Go type | Required | Default | Description                                                                                                   |
| --------- | ------ | ------ | ------- | :------: | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `name`    | path   | string | string  |     ✓    |         | Mute timing name                                                                                              |
| `version` | query  | string | string  |          |         | Current version of the resource. Used for optimistic concurrency validation. Keep empty to bypass validation. |

### <span id="route-set-user-receiver-permission"></span> Set permissions for a user on a specific receiver. (*RouteSetUserReceiverPermission*)

```
POST /api/access-control/receivers/:uid/users/:userID
```

This endpoint sets or removes permissions for a specific user on a receiver. To remove a permission, send an empty string as the permission value.

> **Note**
>
> This endpoint requires Grafana Enterprise and the user must have the `receivers.permissions:write` permission for the specified receiver.

#### Parameters

| Name     | Source | Type   | Go type | Required | Default | Description                                                                           |
| -------- | ------ | ------ | ------- | :------: | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `uid`    | path   | string | string  |     ✓    |         | UID of the receiver                                                                   |
| `userID` | path   | int64  | int64   |     ✓    |         | ID of the user to assign permissions to                                               |
| `body`   | body   | object | object  |     ✓    |         | JSON body with `permission` field: `View`, `Edit`, `Admin`, or `""` (empty to remove) |

#### All responses

| Code          | Status      | Description        | Has headers | Schema           |
| ------------- | ----------- | ------------------ | :---------: | ---------------- |
| [200](#route) | OK          | Permission updated |             | [schema](#route) |
| [400](#route) | Bad Request | Invalid request    |             | [schema](#route) |
| [403](#route) | Forbidden   | Permission denied  |             | [schema](#route) |

#### Responses

##### <span id="route-set-user-receiver-permission-200"></span> 200 - Permission updated

Status: OK

###### <span id="route-set-user-receiver-permission-200-schema"></span> Schema

```json
{
  "message": "Permission updated"
}
```

### <span id="route-post-alert-rule"></span> Create a new alert rule. (*RoutePostAlertRule*)

```
POST /api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules
```

> **Warning**
>
> This API is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Use the [Grafana App Platform alerting APIs](https://editor.swagger.io/?url=https://raw.githubusercontent.com/grafana/grafana/main/packages/grafana-openapi/src/apis/rules.alerting.grafana.app-v1beta1.json) instead: `/apis/rules.alerting.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/alertrules` for alert rules and `/apis/rules.alerting.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/recordingrules` for recording rules.

This action creates a new alert rule.

The provenance (`X-Disable-Provenance`) of the new rule must match the provenance configured for its rule group. Mixing provisioned and unprovisioned alert rules within the same rule group is not allowed.

#### Parameters

| Name                         | Source | Type                                          | Go type                       | Required | Default | Description                                               |
| ---------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | :------: | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| `X-Disable-Provenance: true` | header | string                                        | string                        |          |         | Allows editing of provisioned resources in the Grafana UI |
| `Body`                       | body   | [ProvisionedAlertRule](#provisionedalertrule) | `models.ProvisionedAlertRule` |          |         |                                                           |

#### All responses

| Code          | Status      | Description          | Has headers | Schema           |
| ------------- | ----------- | -------------------- | :---------: | ---------------- |
| [201](#route) | Created     | ProvisionedAlertRule |             | [schema](#route) |
| [400](#route) | Bad Request | ValidationError      |             | [schema](#route) |

### <span id="route-reset-policy-tree"></span> Clears the notification policy tree. (*RouteResetPolicyTree*)

```
DELETE /api/v1/provisioning/policies
```

> **Warning**
>
> This API is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Use the [Grafana App Platform alerting APIs](https://editor.swagger.io/?url=https://raw.githubusercontent.com/grafana/grafana/main/packages/grafana-openapi/src/apis/notifications.alerting.grafana.app-v1beta1.json) instead: `/apis/notifications.alerting.grafana.app/v1beta1/namespaces/{namespace}/routingtrees` for notification policies.

#### All responses

| Code          | Status   | Description | Has headers | Schema           |
| ------------- | -------- | ----------- | :---------: | ---------------- |
| [202](#route) | Accepted | Ack         |             | [schema](#route) |

#### Responses

##### <span id="route-reset-policy-tree-202"></span> 202 - Ack

Status: Accepted

###### <span id="route-reset-policy-tree-202-schema"></span> Schema

[Ack](#ack)

### <span id="embedded-contact-point"></span> EmbeddedContactPoint

EmbeddedContactPoint is the contact point type used by Grafana-managed alerts.

When creating a contact point, the `EmbeddedContactPoint.name` property determines if the new contact point is added to an existing one. In the UI, contact points with the same name are grouped together under a single contact point.

**Properties**

| Name                    | Type          | Go type | Required | Default | Description                                                                        | Example                 |
| ----------------------- | ------------- | ------- | :------: | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| `disableResolveMessage` | boolean       | `bool`  |          |         |                                                                                    | false                   |
| `name`                  | string        | string  |          |         | `name` groups multiple contact points with the same name in the UI.                | `webhook_1`             |
| `provenance`            | string        | string  |          |         |                                                                                    |                         |
| `settings`              | [JSON](#json) | JSON    |     ✓    |         |                                                                                    |                         |
| `type`                  | string        | string  |     ✓    |         |                                                                                    | `webhook`               |
| `uid`                   | string        | string  |          |         | UID is the unique identifier of the contact point. The UID can be set by the user. | `my_external_reference` |

### <span id="record"></span> Record

> Record defines the configuration for a recording rule, which pre-computes query results and saves them as a new time series metric.

**Properties**

| Name                    | Type   | Go type | Required | Default | Description                                                                                                          | Example                        |
| ----------------------- | ------ | ------- | :------: | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
| `metric`                | string | string  |     ✓    |         | The name of the new metric to create. Must be a valid Prometheus metric name with no whitespace.                     | `http_requests:rate5m:sum`     |
| `from`                  | string | string  |     ✓    |         | The query reference ID (refId) to use as the source for the recorded metric.                                         | `A`                            |
| `target_datasource_uid` | string | string  |          |         | UID of the Prometheus-compatible data source to write results to. Falls back to configured default if not specified. | `my-prometheus-datasource-uid` |
